Binary local search might be more promising to get exact lower discrete values. Let's explore implement efficient binary local search for n around 120/160/256.

Binary local search details:
- n even, N=n//2, x int8 0/1.
- Compute plus and minus arrays length n (d=0 plus=minus? for binary d=0 0). plus[d] for positive d, minus[d]. maxC = max(plus,minus)/N.
- For cost maybe integer Kmax = max(plus,minus). Objective minimize Kmax. To avoid high degeneracy, cost = Kmax + ε * (number of lags at Kmax) + maybe weighted sum top few scaled in unit counts.
- Local moves: swap a 1 and a 0 (maintain sum). For each candidate pair, compute new Kmax quickly.
   Naive: all pairs O(N^2) per iteration too slow for n=256 (4M*O?). But n=120 (3600 pairs) manageable. We can do best-improvement over all pairs if O(1) update? Need compute effect on all lags? Could precompute for each pair? Maybe too memory. But n=120, O(n) per pair =>432k operations per full pass; if 1e4 passes =>4e9 too high in Python.
   We can use stochastic local search: pick random swap, evaluate via recompute? O(n^2) recompute no. Need incremental O(n) per eval. If 1e6 swaps, n=120 O120M maybe okay in optimized NumPy vectorized? Python loops maybe slow.
   Could vectorize evaluation of one swap for all d using arrays? For given p (1->0), q (0->1), updated plus/minus changes can be computed by loops over d or using vector operations length n. In Python, vectorized length n per swap may be okay for n=256 and 100k swaps (25M ops). But overhead of creating arrays each swap high. Need efficient.
- Alternatively precompute for each position i contributions? Each pair effect on lag d: If p: removing 1. For plus[d] where p is first index and p+d<n: old contribution h_p(1-h_{p+d}) = 1-h_{p+d}; new 0. If p is second index p-d>=0: old (1-h_{p-d})*1; new (1-0)*1=1, so plus[d] increases by h_{p-d}? Wait if p second index, term x_{p-d}(1-x_p) old = 1 - x_{p-d}, new = 1 - 0 =1, increase by x_{p-d}. For minus similarly with roles. For q adding 1: analogous. Total delta for pair can be computed by summing over d where p or q participates. That's O(n) scalar.
   Could precompute for each position p arrays of deltas for flipping it (1<->0) for each d, conditional on neighbor values?
```
grad = np.zeros(n)
grad += w0*(1-2*h)
for d in range(1,n):
    wP=w_plus[d]*dx; wM=w_minus[d]*dx
    if wP or wM:
       # plus
       if wP:
          # i=0..n-d-1: (1-h[i+d]) -> add to grad[0:n-d]
          grad[:n-d] += wP*(1-h[d:])
          # i=d..n-1: -h[i-d] -> add to grad[d:]
          grad[d:] += -wP*h[:-d]
       if wM:
          grad[:n-d] += -wM*h[d:]
          grad[d:] += wM*(1-h[:-d])
```

```
def compute_vals(h, dx):
    n=len(h); c0 = np.dot(h,1-h)
    plus = np.zeros(n); minus = np.zeros(n) # index d
    for d in range(1,n):
       plus[d] = np.dot(h[:n-d], 1-h[d:])
       minus[d] = np.dot(1-h[:n-d], h[d:])
    vals = np.concatenate(([c0], plus[1:], minus[1:]))
    return vals, c0, plus, minus
```

```
def top_weights(vals, M=16, T=0.02):
    order = np.argpartition(vals, -M)[-M:]
    top = vals[order]
    # weights proportional to exp((top - topmax)/T)
    weights = np.exp((top - top.max())/T)

    return dict? map to arrays w0, wplus, wminus
```

```
if w0: grad += w0*(1-2*h)
```

Vectorized top pairs:
```
def select_pairs_by_gradient(grad, h, K):
    n=len(h)
    if n <= 800:
       diff = grad[:,None] - grad[None,:]
       hi = h[:,None]; hj = h[None,:]
       amount = np.where(diff > 0, np.minimum(hi, 1.0-hj), np.minimum(hj, 1.0-hi))
       score = np.abs(diff) * amount
       np.fill_diagonal(score, 0.0)
       flat = score.ravel()
       # exclude zero
       if K >= flat.size: idx = np.arange(flat.size)
       else:
          # If many zeros, partition top K
          idx = np.argpartition(flat, -K)[-K:]
          idx = idx[np.argsort(flat[idx])[::-1]]
       pairs=[]
       for flat_idx in idx:
          i,j = divmod(flat_idx,n)
          if i<j and score[i,j]>1e-12:
             if grad[i] > grad[j]: high=i; low=j; delta=-min(h[i],1-h[j])
             else: delta=min(1-h[i],h[j])
             if abs(delta)>1e-8: pairs.append((i,j,delta))
       return pairs[:K]
    else:
       heap=[]; for i... but likely not.
```
But `np.where(diff>0, np.minimum(hi,1-hj), np.minimum(hj,1-hi))` may allow amount positive when high has no mass? min handles. If grad[i]>grad[j], moving from i to j requires i has h to remove and j has capacity; if h[i]=0 or 1-h[j]=0 amount 0. Good.

```
def pair_best_move(h, i, j, D, current_c, max_grid=0):
    fracs = [0.5,1.0,0.25] # maybe sorted by expected?
    best_h=None; best_c=current_c
    for f in fracs:
       nh = h.copy(); nh[i]+=D*f; nh[j]-=D*f
       c = eval_c5(nh)
       if c < best_c - 1e-12: best_c=c; best_h=nh.copy(); best_f=f
    if best_h is not None and quick_grid:
       # fine grid over fracs maybe 13 values [0,1], or around best_f span 0.4
       a = max(0.0, best_f-0.3); b = min(1.0, best_f+0.3)
       for ff in np.linspace(a,b,13): ...
       # also include 0? no
    return best_h,best_c
```

```
          if newh is not None:
             h=newh; c=newc; update best; moved=True
```

```
class FuzzyState:
    def __init__(self, h_fixed, free_idx, free_target, dx, n, T_log, P):

        self.free = free_idx

    def full_h(self, x):
        h = self.b.copy()
        h[self.free] = x
        return h
    def obj_grad(self, y):
        x = np.empty(len(self.free))
        x[:-1] = y
        x[-1] = self.T - y.sum()
        # penalty
        x_pen = x
        obj = 0; grad_x = np.zeros_like(x)
        if any out:

           obj += self.P*(np.sum(below*below)+np.sum(above*above))
```

```
def project_to_sum(y, target):
    y=np.asarray(y,float)
    if target<=0: return np.zeros_like(y)
    if target>=len(y): return np.ones_like(y)
    lo=float(np.min(y)); hi=float(np.max(y))
    # f(lambda)=sum clip(y-lambda,0,1) decreasing in lambda
    # need f=target. Initially if target < sum_clip(y,0,1)? Actually f(0)=sum clip(y,0,1). Could target outside? We'll set lo=-10, hi=10.
    lo = float(np.min(y)) - 2.0; hi = float(np.max(y)) + 2.0
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       val = np.clip(y - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum()
       if val > target: lo = mid  # need larger lambda to reduce sum
       else: hi = mid
    z = np.clip(y - mid,0,1)
    # adjust residual on coordinates with 0<z<1
    diff = target - z.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-14:
       mask = (z>1e-12)&(z<1-1e-12)
       if mask.any(): z[mask] += diff/mask.sum()
       else:
          # distribute to extremes
          idxs = np.argsort(z)
          for idx in idxs: ...
    return np.clip(z,0,1)
```
Need after distribution ensure sum. Simpler: Use sorting exact projection? Binary 80 iterations should be enough; residual small. If target exactly, okay.

`top_weights_sparse(vals, M)`:
```
def top_weights_sparse(vals, M):
    n=len(vals); n_half=(n-1)//2
    w0=0.0; wp=np.zeros(n_half+1); wm=np.zeros(n_half+1)
    L=vals.size
    if L<=M: order=np.arange(L)
    else: order=np.argpartition(vals, -M)[-M:]
    top=vals[order]
    if top.size==0: return w0,wp,wm
    tmax=top.max()
    # choose T based on spread
    spread = top.max() - top.min()
    T = max(1e-5, 0.5*spread if spread>1e-8 else 0.005)
    weights = np.exp((top - tmax)/T)
    weights /= weights.sum()
    for idx, w in zip(order, weights):
       if idx == n_half: w0 += w
       elif idx < n_half: d = n_half - idx; wp[d] += w
       else: d = idx - n_half; wm[d] += w
    return w0, wp, wm
```
Sparse arrays length n (index d). Index 0 unused for plus/minus. Good.

`weighted_gradient_sparse(h, w0, wp, wm, dx)`:
```
grad=np.zeros_like(h)
if w0: grad += w0*(1-2*h)
n=len(h)
d_list_plus = np.nonzero(wp)[0]
for d in d_list_plus:
   if d<=0 or d>=n: continue
   w=wp[d]*dx
   m=n-d
   grad[:m] += w*(1-h[d])
   grad[d:] += -w*h[:m]
d_list_minus=...
```
Need `h[d]` length m; `1-h[d]` etc. Good.

`weighted_gradient_dense(h, weights, dx)`: weights vector same length as vals. Map to w0, wp, wm dense and call same? Could build arrays O(n) and loop all. Use same function with dense arrays; nonzero all. For performance, we can implement a dedicated loop over d where weights_idx !=0. But okay.
```
def weighted_gradient_from_weights(h, weights, dx):
    n=len(h); L=2*n-1; mid=n-1
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    w=weights[mid]; if w: grad += w*(1-2*h)
    for d in range(1,n):
       wp = weights[mid-d] if w else? Actually plus[d]=vals[mid-d]; minus[d]=vals[mid+d]
       wp=weights[mid-d]; wm=weights[mid+d]
       if wp==0 and wm==0: continue
       m=n-d
       if wp:
          grad[:m] += wp*dx*(1-h[d:])
          grad[d:] += -wp*dx*h[:m]
       if wm:
          grad[:m] += -wm*dx*h[d:]
          grad[d:] += wm*dx*(1-h[:m])
    return grad
```
This loops n all. For dense, easier.
For sparse, we can pass weights vector and only nonzero? But building weights vector for top M maybe okay: use zeros L, set top weights, call dense. This loops all d but skipping zeros (if condition). For M=32, condition check 511 times, still okay. Simpler. We'll use one function `weighted_gradient(h, weights, dx)` with weights array same length. `if wp + wm != 0`. For dense all nonzero. Good.
Top weights sparse can produce weights vector:
```
def top_weights_vector(vals, M):
   L=len(vals); weights=np.zeros(L)
   order=...; top=...; w=...; weights[order]+=w
   return weights
```
Then gradient uses weights. This is simpler and less bug-prone. For fuzzy objective, weights from all vals softmax.
Gradient function:
```
def weighted_gradient(h, weights, dx):
    n=len(h); mid=n-1
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    w0=weights[mid]
    if w0 != 0.0: grad += w0*(1.0-2.0*h)
    for d in range(1,n):
       wp=weights[mid-d]; wm=weights[mid+d]
       s=wp+wm
       if s == 0.0: continue
       m=n-d
       if wp != 0.0:
          wpdx=wp*dx
          grad[:m] += wpdx*(1.0 - h[d:])
          grad[d:] += -wpdx*h[:m]
       if wm != 0.0:
          wmdx=wm*dx
          grad[:m] += -wmdx*h[d:]
          grad[d:] += wmdx*(1.0 - h[:m])
    return grad
```
Note `h[:m]` used both for first and second; `h[d:]` length m. Good.

Top weights vector:
```
def top_weights_vector(vals, M, T=None):
    L=vals.size; weights=np.zeros(L)
    if L <= M: order=np.arange(L)
    else: order=np.argpartition(vals, -M)[-M:]
    top=vals[order]
    if top.size:
       if T is None:
          spread = top.max()-top.min()
          T = max(1e-6, 0.75*spread if spread>1e-8 else 0.005)
       w=np.exp((top-top.max())/T)
       if w.sum()>0: w/=w.sum()
       weights[order]=w
    return weights
```
If vals all zeros, weights uniform? top max 0, spread 0, T=0.005, w=1 each, uniform among top. Good.
For all-lags softmax in fuzzy:
```
def all_weights_vector(vals, T):
    m=vals.max(); w=np.exp((vals-m)/T); s=w.sum(); return w/s if s>0 else None
```

`select_pairs(grad, h, K)`:
Need return list of tuples `(i,j,D)`. D is directed max delta (h_i += D, h_j -= D). Use gradient high/low.
For n small vectorized. For n up to maybe 1000. Use memory. Implementation robust:
```
def select_pairs_by_gradient(grad, h, K):
    n=len(h)
    pairs=[]
    if n > 1200:
       # nested with heap
       import heapq
       heap=[]
       for i in range(n):
          gi=grad[i]; hi=h[i]
          for j in range(i+1,n):
             gj=grad[j]
             diff=gi-gj
             if diff > 0:
                amount = min(hi, 1.0-h[j])
                D = -amount
             else:
                amount = min(1.0-hi, h[j])
                D = amount
             if amount > 1e-9:
                score = abs(diff)*amount
                if len(heap) < K: heapq.heappush(heap, (-score,i,j,D))
                elif score > -heap[0][0]: heapq.heapreplace(...)
       return [(-s,i,j,D) for ...]
    else:
       diff = grad[:,None] - grad[None,:]
       hi = h[:,None]; hj = h[None,:]
       # if diff>0 high i, low j, D=-min(hi,1-hj); else high j, low i, D=min(1-hi,hj)
       amount = np.where(diff > 0.0, np.minimum(hi, 1.0-hj), np.minimum(1.0-hi, hj))
       sign = np.where(diff > 0.0, -1.0, 1.0)
       D = sign * amount
       score = np.abs(diff) * amount
       np.fill_diagonal(score, 0.0)
       flat_score = score.ravel()
       if K >= flat_score.size:
          idxs = np.argsort(flat_score)[::-1]
       else:
          idxs = np.argpartition(flat_score, -K)[-K:]
          # sort selected by score desc
          sel_scores = flat_score[idxs]
          order = np.argsort(-sel_scores)
          idxs = idxs[order]
       for flat_index in idxs:
          i,j = divmod(int(flat_index), n)
          if i == j: continue
          dval = float(D[i,j])
          if abs(dval) > 1e-9:
             pairs.append((int(i), int(j), dval))
          if len(pairs)>=K: break
       return pairs
```
Memory diff D amount score 3 arrays plus hi/hj. For n=1024, each 1M float 8MB, total ~32-40MB okay. For n=2048, ~128MB each maybe high. We limit n maybe <=1024 in polish. If we upsample to 2048, may be memory heavy. We can set in n>1000 use nested. Good.

`pair_best_move`:
```
def pair_best_move(h, i, j, D, current_c, dx, quick_fracs=(0.5,1.0,0.25), refine_counts=11):
    n=len(h)
    if abs(D) < 1e-10: return None, current_c
    best_h=None; best_c=current_c; best_f=0.0
    for f in quick_fracs:
       delta = D * f
       nh = h.copy()
       nh[i] += delta
       nh[j] -= delta
       # if out of bounds due to roundoff, adjust
       vi=nh[i]; vj=nh[j]
       if vi < -1e-12 or vi > 1+1e-12 or vj < -1e-12 or vj > 1+1e-12: continue
       c = c5_eval(nh, dx)
       if c < best_c - 1e-12:
          best_c=c; best_h=nh.copy(); best_f=f
    if best_h is not None and refine_counts > 0:
       # refine over interval [max(0,best_f-0.4), min(1,best_f+0.4)] plus 0,1? Include quicks
       a = max(0.0, best_f - 0.35)
       b = min(1.0, best_f + 0.35)
       grid = np.linspace(a,b,refine_counts)
       # include 0? final not needed; include 1
       for f in grid:
          if abs(f-best_f)<1e-6: continue
          delta = D * f
          nh = h.copy()
          nh[i] += delta; nh[j] -= delta
          if nh[i] < -1e-12 or nh[i] >1+1e-12 or nh[j] < -1e-12 or nh[j] >1+1e-12: continue
          c = c5_eval(nh, dx)
          if c < best_c - 1e-12:
             best_c=c; best_h=nh.copy(); best_f=f
    return best_h, best_c
```
This evaluates many copies. Could optimize by not copying for refine if evaluate uses `h`? Must copy. Fine.
If current_c has floating noise, tolerance 1e-12. Good.

`polish_coordinate`:
Inputs h, rng, deadline, maybe start_c. It will update local best and return best h,c. It should respect deadline, leaving reserve maybe.
Pseudo:
```
def coordinate_polish(h, rng, deadline, max_evals_per_pass=???, coords_only=True):
    n=len(h); N=n//2; dx=2.0/n
    h=ensure_h(h)
    c=c5_eval(h,dx)
    no_improve=0
    M= int(16 + min(32, n//16))? M=32
    passes=0
    while time.time() < deadline - 30:
       vals = np.maximum(np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')*dx,0)
       weights = top_weights_vector(vals, M)
       grad = weighted_gradient(h, weights, dx)
       K = max(50, min(400, 200000//max(1,n)))
       pairs = select_pairs_by_gradient(grad,h,K)
       if not pairs: break
       moved=False
       pass_moves=0
       for (i,j,D) in pairs:
          if time.time() > deadline - 30: break
          newh,newc = pair_best_move(h,i,j,D,c,dx, refine_counts=9 if n<256 else 5)
          if newh is not None:
             h=newh; c=newc; moved=True; pass_moves+=1
             if pass_moves >= 8: break
       if moved:
          no_improve=0
          passes+=1
       else:
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve >= 2:
             # perturbation hill climb
             hp,c_p = hill_climb_random(h,c,rng,dx,n,N,deadline, steps=30, T=0.0015)
             if c_p < c - 1e-10:
                h=hp; c=c_p; no_improve=0
             elif c_p < c + 0.005: # accept worsened to escape? We should keep original best separately
                # To eventually find improvement, we can work from perturbed but keep best_h outside. Simpler: set h=hp,c=c_p if not too worse, no_improve=0, but final best stored outside.
                # But if we return local best, we need preserve best.
                # Let function keep best_h,best_c inside.
             else:
                # maybe give up this start
                break
       # update best
       if c < best_c: best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
    return best_h,best_c
```
Need track `best_h,best_c` inside. When perturb worsens, we can set working h=hp,c=c_p but best remains. If after perturb coordinate finds improvement below best, updates. If time ends, return best.
We should not let working c worsen too much (e.g., > best+0.02) because hard to recover. Use `if c_p < c + 0.004` maybe. Since target margin small, allow 0.002. Or accept if random hill returns a better than original. Simpler: only accept perturbation if it improves, else break. But then no escape. We can have a "restart" with original plus small random noise and then coordinate; keep best if not worse too much. Let's implement:
```
if no_improve>=2:
   hp = add_random_noise(h, rng, scale=0.05, N)
   # run a short random hill climb allowing accepting improvements only
   hp,c_hp = random_hill(hp,rng,deadline,min(60, ...))
   if c_hp < c:
      h=hp; c=c_hp; no_improve=0
   else:
      # try a few more no_improve then break
      no_improve+=1
      if no_improve>4: break
```
This doesn't accept worse. Escape via random noise that immediately improves? Unlikely. For true plateau, need accept worse. Coordinate descent may still find within noise if c_hp slightly worse but better basin. We can accept if `c_hp < c + 0.001` (small). Then working worsens slightly but may improve. Keep best.

Random hill function:
```
def random_hill_climb(h, rng, dx, n, N, max_steps, deadline, T=None):
    c=c5_eval(h,dx)
    for step in range(max_steps):
       if time>deadline-5: break
       # pick two indices: one high, one low or random
       high_mask = h > 0.5 + 1e-4; low_mask = h < 0.5 - 1e-4
       if high_mask.any() and low_mask.any():
          i=rng.choice(np.nonzero(high_mask)[0]); j=rng.choice(np.nonzero(low_mask)[0])
       else:
          i,j = rng.choice(n,2,replace=False)
       if grad? no. Determine possible swap direction: if h[i]>=h[j], D=-min(h[i],1-h[j]) else D=min(1-h[i],h[j])
       if abs(D)<1e-8: continue
       # maybe partial random fraction
       f = rng.uniform(0.3,1.0)
       delta = D*f
       nh = h.copy(); nh[i]+=delta; nh[j]-=delta
       c_new=c5_eval(nh,dx)
       if c_new < c or (T is not None and rng.random() < math.exp((c-c_new)/T) if c_new>c else True):
           h=nh; c=c_new
    return h,c
```
Annealing probability: if c_new > c, accept if exp((c - c_new)/T) > random. T maybe 0.002. Need if c_new-c large, avoid overflow; use `delta_c = c_new - c; accept if delta_c < 0 or (T>0 and rng.random() < math.exp(-delta_c/T))`.
If T=0, only improvements.
This random hill can be used as perturbation with T=0.001.

But `rng.choice` on arrays each step may be slow if many. For n small okay.

Potential issue: top_weights_vector uses `top_weights` with T based on spread of top M. If top M all equal, T=0.005; weights uniform. Good.

But if we use global `_fuzzy_state`, need class. Top-level.
```
class FuzzyState:
    def __init__(self, b, free, target, dx, Tlog, P):
       self.b=np.asarray(b,float).copy(); ...
       self._last_y=None; self._last_obj=None; self._last_grad=None
    def _x_from_y(self, y):
       x=np.empty(self.k); x[:-1]=y; x[-1]=self.target - y.sum(); return x
    def obj(self, y):
       x=self._x_from_y(y)
       return self.compute(x)[0]
    def jac(self, y):
       x=self._x_from_y(y)
       return self.compute(x)[1]
    def compute(self, x):
       # compute with clipping for smooth part
       xc=np.clip(x,0,1)
       h=self.b.copy(); h[self.free]=xc
       vals=np.maximum(np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')*self.dx,0)
       m=vals.max()
       w=np.exp((vals-m)/self.Tlog)
       s=w.sum()
       if s==0: weights=np.full(vals.size,1/vals.size)
       else: weights=w/s
       obj = m + self.Tlog * math.log(s)  # since logsum = log(s)+? Actually logsumexp(vals/T)=log(s)+ (m-m)/T? If w=exp((vals-m)/T), sum w = exp(logsum - m/T). logsum = m/T + log(s). So T*logsum = m + T*log(s). Yes.
       grad_h = weighted_gradient(h, weights, self.dx)
       # penalty on x (unclipped)
       below = np.minimum(x,0.0); above=np.maximum(x-1.0,0.0)
       pen = self.P*(np.sum(below*below)+np.sum(above*above))
       grad_x = grad_h[self.free].copy()
       grad_x += self.P*(-2.0*below + 2.0*above)
       return obj+pen, grad_x
```
But note `grad_h` computed on clipped h; if x out, derivative of clipped h is zero inside out region, penalty active. Fine.

```
class FuzzyState:
    def objective(self,y):
        if self._last_y is not None and np.array_equal(np.asarray(y), self._last_y): return self._last_obj
        obj,grad = self._compute(np.asarray(y,float))
        self._last_y=np.asarray(y).copy(); self._last_obj=obj; self._last_grad=grad; return obj
```

```
def upsample_h(h, new_n):
    old_n=len(h); rng? no
    if new_n % old_n ==0:
       factor = new_n//old_n
       return np.repeat(h, factor)
    else:
       # linear interpolation at cell centers
       x_old = np.arange(old_n) + 0.5
       x_new = np.arange(new_n) + 0.5
       # linearly interpolate periodic? Use np.interp with left/right? For step function, interpolate at centers.
       h_new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, h, left=h[0], right=h[-1])
       return h_new
```

```
    n=len(h); best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c5_eval(...)
    if n > 1024: return best_h,best_c
```

`random_hill_climb`: Need avoid lambdas. Use `np.nonzero` each step. For n=120 fine. Use `math.exp` guarded. If T small, probability. If T=0, no worse. Code:
```
def random_hill_climb(h, rng, dx, max_steps, phase_deadline, temperature=0.0):
    h=h.copy(); c=c5_eval(h,dx); n=len(h)
    for _ in range(max_steps):
       if time.time() > phase_deadline - 2: break
       high_idx = np.flatnonzero(h > 0.5 + 1e-4)
       low_idx = np.flatnonzero(h < 0.5 - 1e-4)
       if high_idx.size and low_idx.size:
          i=int(rng.choice(high_idx)); j=int(rng.choice(low_idx))
       else:
          i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
          if i==j: continue
       if h[i] > h[j]:
          amount = min(h[i], 1.0 - h[j]); D=-amount
       else:
          amount = min(1.0 - h[i], h[j]); D=amount
       if abs(D) < 1e-8: continue
       f = float(rng.uniform(0.3,1.0))
       nh = h.copy(); nh[i] += D*f; nh[j] -= D*f
       c_new = c5_eval(nh,dx)
       if c_new < c:
          h=nh; c=c_new
       elif temperature>0:
          diff = c_new - c
          logp = -diff/temperature
          if logp > 700 or rng.random() < math.exp(logp): h=nh; c=c_new
    return h,c
```
If c_new huge, diff/T large positive, logp negative large, math.exp underflows to 0; okay. If diff negative handled. If logp > 0? diff negative already. If logp > 700? not for worse. For smaller guard `logp < -700` skip.

`add_noise` maybe not needed; random hill from current with temperature can accept worse. But if starting from local optimum, random moves likely small improvements? It may not change basin unless accept sequence of worse moves. Temperature annealing can help. We'll use random hill with T=0.001 for 50 steps. It returns best local after path (not necessarily original best path? It tracks h walking, not best seen. If path ends worse but intermediate better, we might lose. Modify to track best_seen and return best_seen if better, else maybe final if better? For perturbation, we want possibly end in different basin even if worse than original but better than alternatives. If we return best_seen (improving only), no escape. So return final h,c, not best, but coordinate polish from that may improve. However if final worse too much, may waste. We'll in perturb decide: if c_p < c + 0.003, accept working; else not. But keep global best. In coordinate_polish, after random hill, if c_p < c + 0.003: h=hp; c=c_p; no_improve=0 (but if c_p > best_c, global best unaffected). If c_p too bad, break/retry. This allows escape.

Potential issue: We might output a h with values not satisfying `sum(h) == n/2` due to projection. We can final enforce. Function `finalize_h(h, n)`:
```
def finalize_h(h, n):
    N=n/2.0
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    # iterative projection to sum
    for _ in range(5):
       h=project_to_sum(h,N)
       break
    # If all values close to 0/1, snap
    tol = 1e-7
    nsnap = np.sum(h>=1-tol) + np.sum(h<=tol)
    if nsnap == n:
       b = (h>0.5).astype(float)
       if abs(b.sum()-N) < 1e-9:
          h = b
       else:
          # adjust nearest to 0.5
          b = round_to_sum(h,N)
          h = b
    return h
```
But if continuous, don't snap unless all near binary. If snapping changes C, evaluate? We can in run try both `h` and `h_snap` and choose lower c if sum exact. But if h continuous c slightly lower than binary, choose continuous.

Add `projected_gradient_step(h, c, dx, deadline)` inside coordinate_polish:
```
vals=...; weights=top_weights_vector(vals,M);
g=weighted_gradient(h,weights,dx);
g = g - g.mean()
gn = np.linalg.norm(g, ord=np.inf)
if gn>1e-12:
   direc = g/gn  # perhaps also g/np.linalg.norm(g,2)
   for tau in [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0]:
       y = h - tau*direc
       yh = project_to_sum(y,N)
       ch = c5_eval(yh)

```

`projected_gradient_step` needs `N` and h. It can use weights from current vals; if weights uniform top M. It returns best of several tau. Implementation:
```
def projected_gradient_step(h, c, dx, weights, N, max_evals=6):
    n=len(h); g=weighted_gradient(h,weights,dx)
    g = g - np.mean(g)
    scale = np.linalg.norm(g, ord=np.inf)
    if scale < 1e-12: return h,c
    directing = g / scale
    taus = [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0]
    best_h=None; best_c=c
    for tau in taus:
       y = h - tau*directing
       yh = project_to_sum(y, float(N))
       ch = c5_eval(yh, dx)
       if ch < best_c - 1e-12: best=...
    return best_h,best_c
```
If no improvement, no change. This is derivative-free line search on surrogate.

Constructing sinusoidal start:
```
def structured_start(n, rng):
    N=n//2
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
    score = np.zeros(n)
    # deterministic frequencies plus random phases
    for f in [3,5,7,11]:
       phase = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
       score += np.sin(2*np.pi*f*x + phase)/f
    order=np.argsort(-score)
    h=np.zeros(n); h[order[:N]]=1.0
    return h
```

Now, potential bug in `project_to_sum`: The binary search updates lo/hi but `mid` variable remains from last iteration. Need ensure if target outside possible due to floating, handle. We can implement more robust:
```
def project_to_sum(y,target):
   y=np.asarray(y,float)
   n=len(y)
   target=float(target)
   if target <= 0: return np.zeros(n)
   if target >= n: return np.ones(n)
   lo = float(np.min(y)) - 2.0
   hi = float(np.max(y)) + 2.0
   for _ in range(80):
      mid=(lo+hi)/2
      val=np.clip(y-mid,0,1).sum()
      if val > target: lo=mid
      else: hi=mid
   z=np.clip(y-(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
   # distribute residual
   diff=target-z.sum()
   if diff !=0:
      # find indices with 0<z<1
      mask=(z>1e-12)&(z<1-1e-12)
      if mask.any(): z[mask]+=diff/mask.sum()
      else:
         # order by z and adjust extremes
         order=np.argsort(np.where(diff>0, -z, z)) # not allowed walrus; do if
         if diff>0:
             for idx in np.argsort(-z):
                add=min(1-z[idx], diff)
                z[idx]+=add; diff-=add
                if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
         else:
             for idx in np.argsort(z):
                sub=min(z[idx], -diff)
                z[idx]-=sub; diff+=sub
                if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
   return np.clip(z,0,1)
```
This ensures sum close. The sorting for residual rarely used.

Now, if target not integer and n small, works.

Now, `round_to_sum` with h values NaN? no.

`c5_eval` with finite check:
```
def c5_eval(h, dx):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if not np.isfinite(h).all(): return 1e100
    vals = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full') * dx
    if vals.size == 0: return 1e100
    return float(np.max(np.maximum(vals, 0.0)))
```
`np.isfinite(h).all()` returns np.bool; okay.

`project_to_sum` as above.

`ensure_h`:
```
def ensure_h(h):
    h=np.asarray(h,dtype=float).copy()
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    N=float(h.size/2)
    if abs(float(h.sum())-N) > 1e-8:
       h=project_to_sum(h,N)
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    # final tiny correction
    diff=N - h.sum()
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       # project again
       h=project_to_sum(h,N)
    return h
```

`round_to_sum`:
```
def round_to_sum(h,N):
    n=h.size
    order=np.argsort(-h, kind='mergesort')
    b=np.zeros(n)
    Nint=int(round(N))
    if Nint<0: Nint=0
    if Nint>n: Nint=n
    b[order[:Nint]]=1.0
    return b
```
N expected int.

`top_weights_vector` and `all_weights_vector`:
```
def top_weights_vector(vals, M):
    L=vals.size; weights=np.zeros(L)
    if M <=0: return weights
    if L <= M: order=np.arange(L)
    else:
       M=min(M,L)
       order=np.argpartition(vals, -M)[-M:]
    top=vals[order]
    if top.size == 0: return weights
    spread=float(top.max()-top.min())
    T=max(1e-6, 0.75*spread if spread > 1e-8 else 0.005)
    w=np.exp((top - float(top.max()))/T)
    s=w.sum()
    if s>0:
       weights[order] = w/s
    else:
       weights[order] = 1.0/top.size
    return weights

def all_weights_vector(vals, T):
    m=float(vals.max())
    w=np.exp((vals-m)/T)
    s=w.sum()
    if s>0: return w/s
    return np.full(vals.size, 1.0/vals.size)
```

`weighted_gradient`:
```
def weighted_gradient(h, weights, dx):
    n=int(h.size)
    mid=n-1
    grad=np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if mid < weights.size:
       w0=weights[mid]
       if w0 != 0.0:
          grad += w0*(1.0 - 2.0*h)
    for d in range(1,n):
       idxp=mid-d; idxm=mid+d
       wp = weights[idxp] if idxp >=0 else 0.0
       wm = weights[idxm] if idxm < weights.size else 0.0
       if wp == 0.0 and wm == 0.0: continue
       m=n-d
       if wp != 0.0:
          wpdx=wp*dx
          grad[:m] += wpdx*(1.0 - h[d:])
          grad[d:] += -wpdx*h[:m]
       if wm != 0.0:
          wmdx=wm*dx
          grad[:m] += -wmdx*h[d:]
          grad[d:] += wmdx*(1.0 - h[:m])
    return grad
```
For d loop, `h[:m]` used for second: if d=m? length okay. For minus, second contribution (1-h[i-d]) for i=d..n-1 -> h[:m]. Good.

```
diff=gi-gj
if diff > 0:
   amount = h[i]
   cap = 1.0 - h[j]
   if cap < amount: amount=cap
   D=-amount
else:
   amount = 1.0 - h[i]
   cap = h[j]
   if cap < amount: amount=cap
   D=amount
if amount > 1e-9:
   score=abs(diff)*amount
   if len(heap)<K: heapq.heappush(heap, (-score, i, j, D))
   elif score > -heap[0][0]: heapq.heapreplace(heap, (-score, i, j, D))
```
At end `return [(i,j,D) for neg,i,j,D in heap]` no sorting.
Tie score equal, heap compares i then j then D; D float okay. Good.

```
diff = grad[:,None] - grad[None,:]
hi = h[:,None]; hj=h[None,:]
use_i = diff > 0.0
amount = np.minimum(hi, 1.0 - hj) * use_i + np.minimum(1.0 - hi, hj) * (1.0 - use_i)
# for i==j amount maybe min(h_i,1-h_i), but score diag zero; D diag maybe.
sign = np.where(use_i, -1.0, 1.0)
D = sign * amount
score = np.abs(diff) * amount
np.fill_diagonal(score, 0.0)
flat = score.ravel()
K=min(K, flat.size)
if K <=0: return []
idxs=np.argpartition(flat, -K)[-K:]
sel=flat[idxs]
order=np.argsort(-sel)
idxs=idxs[order]
res=[]
for idx in idxs:
   i=int(idx//n); j=int(idx%n)
   if i==j: continue
   dval=float(D[i,j])
   if abs(dval)>1e-9:
      res.append((i,j,dval))
   if len(res)>=K: break
return res
```

`projected_gradient_step`:
```
def projected_gradient_step(h, c, dx, weights, N, phase_deadline=None):
    g=weighted_gradient(h, weights, dx)
    g = g - float(np.mean(g))
    scale = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
    if scale < 1e-12: return h,c
    direc = g / scale
    taus = [0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, 1.0, 3.0]
    best_h=None; best_c=c
    for tau in taus:
       if phase_deadline is not None and time.time() > phase_deadline - 2: break
       y = h - tau*directing
       yh = project_to_sum(y, float(N))
       ch = c5_eval(yh, dx)
       if ch < best_c - 1e-12:
          best_h=yh.copy(); best_c=ch
    return best_h, best_c
```
If no, returns original. We may also try a smaller number of taus if time.

`random_hill_climb`:
Use `phase_deadline`. For performance, precompute indices? Each step high/low using flatnonzero. For many steps okay.

`coordinate_polish`:
Need pass `phase_deadline`, maybe `max_passes` not necessary. It returns local best.
Pseudo code final:
```
def coordinate_polish(h, rng, phase_deadline):
    n=len(h); N=n//2; dx=2.0/n
    h=ensure_h(h)
    c=c5_eval(h,dx)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
    no_improve=0
    M=int(min(80, max(32, n//4)))
    K=int(max(50, min(300, 200000 // max(1,n))))
    refine_counts = 9 if n <= 256 else 5
    pass_count=0
    while time.time() < phase_deadline - 15:
        vals = np.maximum(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full') * dx, 0.0)
        weights = top_weights_vector(vals, M)
        gh, gc = projected_gradient_step(h, c, dx, weights, N, phase_deadline)
        if gc < c - 1e-11:
           h=gh; c=gc; no_improve=0
           if c < best_c: best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
           continue
        grad = weighted_gradient(h, weights, dx)
        pairs = select_pairs_by_gradient(grad, h, K)
        moved=False
        moves=0
        for (i,j,D) in pairs:
            if time.time() > phase_deadline - 15: break
            newh, newc = pair_best_move(h, i, j, D, c, dx, refine_counts)
            if newh is not None and newc < c - 1e-11:
                h=newh; c=newc; moved=True; moves +=1
                if c < best_c:
                   best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
                if moves >= 8:
                   break
        if moved:
           no_improve=0
        else:
           # random perturbation
           hp, cp = random_hill_climb(h, rng, dx, 40, phase_deadline, temperature=0.0008)
           if cp < c + 0.003 and cp < best_c + 0.01:
               h=hp; c=cp; no_improve=0
           else:
               no_improve += 1
               if no_improve > 4:
                   break
    # ensure final best
    if c5_eval(best_h,dx) < best_c: best_c = c5_eval(best_h,dx)
    return best_h, best_c
```
Note: after accepting random perturbation worse than best_c, best_c remains lower; local working c may be worse. If c < best_c? no. The loop condition uses `c` for pair moves; if c is worse, pair moves are relative to worse c, can still improve. Good.
But if random perturbation makes c much better than best, update best after. We should update best after perturbation:
```
if c < best_c: best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
```
Add after random accept.
If random perturbation not accepted and no_improve>4 break, return best.
Potential issue: If gradient step improves but to a value worse than previous best? It already improves working, if working was worse than best, best_c not updated. okay.

`random_hill_climb` returns final h,c. If it walks worse then better than start but final not better? Could be. We compare cp < c+0.003. okay.

`fuzzy_polish`:
```
def fuzzy_polish(h, rng, phase_deadline):
    n=len(h); N=n//2; dx=2.0/n
    h=ensure_h(h)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c5_eval(h,dx)
    if n > 1024:
       return best_h,best_c
    # full continuous optimization
    temps = [0.02, 0.008, 0.003]
    for Tlog in temps:
       if time.time() > phase_deadline - 25: break
       # start from best_h (maybe add slight noise if T small?)
       start = best_h.copy()
       if Tlog < 0.01 and n <= 512:
           # small noise to escape
           start = start + rng.normal(0.0, 0.01, n)
           start = ensure_h(start)
       maxiter = 80 if n <= 256 else 40
       # Full: b zeros, free all
       free = np.arange(n)
       target = float(N)
       b = np.zeros(n)
       # order pivot: we can keep as is; last variable eliminated. Ensure target interior for last by reordering based on start
       x0 = project_to_sum(start[free], target)
       # choose pivot with max min(x0,1-x0)
       if n > 1:
          pivot = int(np.argmax(np.minimum(x0, 1.0 - x0)))
          perm = np.concatenate((np.arange(0, pivot), np.arange(pivot+1, n), np.array([pivot])))
          free = free[perm]
          x0 = x0[perm]
       state = FuzzyState(b, free, target, dx, Tlog, 1e5)
       _fuzzy_state = state
       y0 = x0[:-1].copy()
       k = n
       opts = {'maxiter': maxiter, 'maxfun': maxiter*8, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-10}
       bounds = [(0.0,1.0) for _ in range(k-1)]
       try:
          res = minimize(_fuzzy_fun, y0, jac=_fuzzy_jac, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options=opts)
          if res.x is not None:
             xres = state.x_from_y(res.x)
             xres = project_to_sum(xres, target)
             hnew = b.copy(); hnew[free] = xres
             cnew = c5_eval(hnew, dx)
             if cnew < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_h = hnew.copy(); best_c = cnew
       except Exception:
          pass
    # subset fuzzy perhaps
    for K in [4,8,16,32,64]:
       if K >= n or time.time() > phase_deadline - 20: break
       if K < 2: continue
       b = round_to_sum(best_h, N)
       free = choose_free_indices(best_h, b, K)
       target = float(b[free].sum())
       if target <= 0.0 or target >= float(K): continue
       x0 = project_to_sum(best_h[free], target)
       pivot = int(np.argmax(np.minimum(x0, 1.0-x0)))
       perm = np.concatenate((np.arange(pivot), np.arange(pivot+1,K), np.array([pivot])))
       free=free[perm]; x0=x0[perm]; target unchanged
       state = FuzzyState(b, free, target, dx, 0.008, 1e5)
       _fuzzy_state = state
       y0=x0[:-1].copy()
       bounds=[(0.0,1.0) for _ in range(K-1)]
       opts={'maxiter':40,'maxfun':320,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-10}
       try:
          res=minimize(...)
          if res.x is not None: ...
       except: pass
    return best_h,best_c
```

`choose_free_indices`:
```
def choose_free_indices(h,b,K):
    n=len(h); K=min(K,n)
    score = np.minimum(h, 1.0-h) + 0.5 * np.abs(h-b)
    # add small random? deterministic
    idx = np.argsort(-score)
    return idx[:K]
```
If K=n, all.

`FuzzyState` class:
```
class FuzzyState:
    def __init__(self,b,free,target,dx,Tlog,P):
       self.b=np.asarray(b,float).copy()
       self.free=np.asarray(free,dtype=int)
       self.target=float(target)
       self.dx=float(dx); self.Tlog=float(Tlog); self.P=float(P)
       self.k=len(self.free)
       self._last_y=None; self._last_obj=None; self._last_grad=None
    def x_from_y(self,y):
       x=np.empty(self.k, dtype=float)
       if self.k == 0:
          x[0]=self.target; return x
       x[:-1]=np.asarray(y,dtype=float)
       x[-1]=self.target - float(np.sum(x[:-1]))
       return x
    def compute(self,y):
       x=self.x_from_y(y)
       # if x has nan return large
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return 1e100, np.zeros(self.k)
       xc=np.clip(x,0.0,1.0)
       h=self.b.copy()
       h[self.free]=xc
       vals=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')*self.dx
       vals=np.maximum(vals,0.0)
       m=float(vals.max()) if vals.size else 0.0
       w=np.exp((vals - m)/self.Tlog)
       s=float(w.sum())
       if s < 1e-300:
          weights=np.full(vals.size, 1.0/vals.size) if vals.size else None
       else:
          weights=w/s
       obj = m + self.Tlog * math.log(s) if s>0 else 0.0
       grad_h = weighted_gradient(h, weights, self.dx)
       below=np.minimum(x,0.0); above=np.maximum(x-1.0,0.0)
       pen = self.P*(float(np.sum(below*below)) + float(np.sum(above*above)))
       grad_x = grad_h[self.free].copy()
       grad_x += self.P*(-2.0*below + 2.0*above)
       return obj+pen, grad_x
    def objective(self,y):
       y=np.asarray(y,dtype=float)
       if self._last_y is not None and np.array_equal(y, self._last_y):
           return self._last_obj
       obj,grad=self.compute(y)
       self._last_y=y.copy(); self._last_obj=obj; self._last_grad=grad
       return obj
    def grad(self,y):
       y=np.asarray(y,dtype=float)
       if self._last_y is not None and np.array_equal(y, self._last_y):
           return self._last_grad
       obj,grad=self.compute(y)
       self._last_y=y.copy(); self._last_obj=obj; self._last_grad=grad
       return grad
```
If k=0, x_from_y error? We won't use. In fuzzy, K>=2 or full n>1. If n=1 skip.
`np.array_equal` if y shape different? no.
If `res.x` is None? handle.

`structured_start`:
```
def structured_start(n, rng):
    N=n//2
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n,endpoint=False)
    score=np.zeros(n)
    freqs=[1,2,3,5,7,11,13]
    for f in freqs:
       phase=float(rng.uniform(0.0,6.28318))
       score += np.sin(2.0*math.pi*f*x + phase)/float(f)
    order=np.argsort(-score)
    h=np.zeros(n); h[order[:N]]=1.0
    return h
```
Could produce too many blocks? top N of sum sines. Good.

`random_binary_start`:
```
def random_binary_start(n, rng):
   N=n//2; idx=rng.choice(n,size=N,replace=False); h=zeros; h[idx]=1; return h
```

Run function detailed:
```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    global _fuzzy_state
    t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+budget_s
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_h=None; best_c=1e100; best_n=0
    # initial
    initial_h = None
    if 'initial_h_values' in globals():
       arr=np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
       if arr.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
          initial_h=arr.copy()
    if initial_h is not None:
       h0=make_even(ensure_h(initial_h))
    else:
       h0=structured_start(240, rng)
    n0=len(h0)
    N0=n0//2
    dx0=2.0/n0
    c0=c5_eval(h0,dx0)
    best_h=h0.copy(); best_c=c0; best_n=n0
    # coordinate polish initial
    remain=deadline-time.time()
    if remain > 30:
       phase = min(time.time()+max(20, remain*0.28), deadline-20)
       h1,c1=coordinate_polish(h0, rng, phase)
       if c1<best_c: best_h=h1; best_c=c1; best_n=len(h1)
    # binary projection polish
    remain=deadline-time.time()
    if remain > 30:
       hb=round_to_sum(best_h, best_n//2)
       cb=c5_eval(hb,2.0/best_n)
       if cb<best_c: best_h=hb; best_c=cb
       phase = min(time.time()+max(15, remain*0.18), deadline-20)
       h2,c2=coordinate_polish(hb,rng,phase)
       if c2<best_c: best_h=h2; best_c=c2; best_n=len(h2)
    # fuzzy polish
    remain=deadline-time.time()
    if remain > 60 and best_n <= 1024:
       phase = min(time.time()+max(30, remain*0.25), deadline-20)
       h3,c3=fuzzy_polish(best_h, rng, phase)
       if c3<best_c: best_h=h3; best_c=c3; best_n=len(h3)
    # upsample and polish
    remain=deadline-time.time()
    if remain > 80:
       new_n = best_n * 2
       if new_n % best_n == 0 and new_n <= 1024:
           h_up = np.repeat(best_h, new_n//best_n)
           h_up = ensure_h(h_up)
           # maybe if repeated, c may increase; coordinate polish
           phase = min(time.time()+max(30, remain*0.25), deadline-20)
           h4,c4=coordinate_polish(h_up, rng, phase)
           if c4<best_c: best_h=h4; best_c=c4; best_n=len(h4)
       # if no multiple, try new_n = 2*best_n? always multiple.
    # alternate structured/fuzzy on different n?
    remain=deadline-time.time()
    if remain > 90:
       # Try a structured start at n = min(512, max(240, best_n*2?))
       nt = 240 if best_n < 240 else min(512, best_n)
       # ensure even
       if nt %2: nt-=1
       hs=structured_start(nt, rng)
       phase = min(time.time()+max(20, remain*0.15), deadline-20)
       h5,c5v=coordinate_polish(hs,rng,phase)
       if c5v<best_c: best_h=h5; best_c=c5v; best_n=len(h5)
    # final fuzzy if time
    remain=deadline-time.time()
    if remain > 40 and best_n <= 1024:
       phase = min(time.time()+max(10, remain*0.4), deadline-10)
       h6,c6=fuzzy_polish(best_h, rng, phase)
       if c6<best_c: best_h=h6; best_c=c6; best_n=len(h6)
    # finalize
    h=finalize_h(best_h)
    n=len(h)
    c=c5_eval(h,2.0/n)
    # try snap if close
    if n%2==0:
       snap=round_to_sum(h, n//2)
       cs=c5_eval(snap,2.0/n)
       if cs < c + 1e-12: # if same or better
           # but if cs worse, keep continuous. We can choose min cs,c.
           if cs < c:
              h=snap; c=cs
    # If c not valid? ensure
    if c < best_c or best_h is None:
       best_c=c
    return (h.tolist()? or h, c, n)
```

```
x = self.x_from_y(y)
if not finite: return large
x_use = np.clip(x,0,1)
```

Global `_interval_state = None`.

`IntervalState`:
```
class IntervalState:
    def __init__(self,K,n_t, T):
       self.K=K; self.n_t=n_t; self.T=T
       self.t = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_t, endpoint=True)
    def intervals(self, raw):
       K=self.K
       ra = raw[:K]; rg = raw[K:]
       a = softmax(ra); g = softmax(rg)
       s=[]; e=[]
       pos = g[0]
       for j in range(K):
           s.append(pos); e.append(pos+a[j])
           pos = e[j] + g[j+1]
       return np.array(s), np.array(e), a, g
    def c_true(self, raw):
       K=self.K; s,e,_,_ = self.intervals(raw); t=self.t
       P=np.zeros(self.n_t); R=np.zeros(self.n_t); Q=np.zeros(self.n_t)
       for j in range(K):
          sj=float(s[j]); ej=float(e[j])
          P += np.maximum(0.0, np.minimum(ej, 2.0 - t) - sj)
       for l in range(K):
          sl=float(s[l]); el=float(e[l])
          R += np.maximum(0.0, np.minimum(el - t, 2.0 - t) - np.maximum(sl - t, 0.0))
       for j in range(K):
          sj=float(s[j]); ej=float(e[j])
          for l in range(K):
             sl=float(s[l]); el=float(e[l])
             lower = np.maximum(sj, sl + t)  # lower also max 0 (sj>=0, sl+t>=0)
             upper = np.minimum(ej, el + t)
             upper = np.minimum(upper, 2.0 - t)
             Q += np.maximum(0.0, upper - lower)
       op = np.maximum(0.0, P - Q)
       om = np.maximum(0.0, R - Q)
       vals = np.maximum(op, om)
       return float(np.max(vals))
    def objective(self, raw):
       # if invalid? params always valid via softmax
       vals? For derivative-free use c_true. For L-BFGS maybe smooth.
       return self.c_true(raw)
```
This computes R formula: For x in [0,2-t], x+t ∈ [sl,el] => x ∈ [sl-t, el-t] ∩ [0,2-t]. upper = min(el-t, 2-t), lower = max(sl-t,0). My code `np.maximum(sl - t, 0.0)` yes.
Q: x ∈ [sj,ej] ∩ [sl+t, el+t] ∩ [0,2-t]. lower = max(sj, sl+t, 0). sj>=0, sl+t>=0, so lower = max(sj, sl+t). upper = min(ej, el+t, 2-t). Good. Need also x>=? if lower > upper length 0. Good.
`P`: x in A_j ∩ [0,2-t]: upper min(e_j,2-t), lower s_j. Since s_j>=0. Good.
This gives continuous integrals over overlap length 2-t. At t=0: R? R[0]=measure A=1, Q=measure A=1, Ominus=0; P=1,Q=1; good. At t=2: P,R,Q=0; C=0.
The maximum over t grid approximates continuous. We can use `n_t=1000`.

Top-level `softmax` function.

Interval optimizer:
```
def optimize_intervals(K, rng, phase_deadline):
    state = IntervalState(K, 1000, None)
    _interval_state = state
    best_raw=None; best_c=inf
    # maybe initial from equal length: raw zeros -> a uniform, g uniform.
    raw0 = np.zeros(2*K+1)
    c0=state.c_true(raw0)
    # random starts 3
    starts=[]
    starts.append(raw0)
    for _ in range(3): starts.append(rng.normal(0,1,2*K+1))
    for raw0 in starts:
       if time>deadline-10 break
       raw=raw0.copy()
       try:
          res=minimize(_interval_obj, raw, method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter': 300, 'xatol':1e-5, 'fatol':1e-7})
          if res.x is not None:
             c=res.fun if finite else state.c_true(res.x)
             if c<best_c: best_c=c; best_raw=res.x.copy()
       except: pass
    return best_raw,best_c
```

```
def interval_params_to_bounds(raw,K):
    a=softmax(raw[:K]); g=softmax(raw[K:])
    s=[]; e=[]
    pos=float(g[0])
    for j in range(K):
       s.append(pos); ee=pos+float(a[j]); e.append(ee); pos=ee+float(g[j+1])
    return s,e
```

Actually `run_interval_search(rng, phase_deadline, best_c)` returns best interval h/c or None. It can optimize K and construct h at n based on time. Let's implement:
```
def run_interval_search(rng, phase_deadline, best_c):
    global _interval_state
    best_h=None; best_c=1e100
    # choose n based on time
    n = 512 if (phase_deadline - time.time()) > 180 else 256
    Ks = [5,4,6,3]
    for K in Ks:
       if time.time() > phase_deadline - 30: break
       # optimize interval parameters
       raw_best, c_cont = optimize_interval_K(K, rng, phase_deadline)
       if c_cont < 0.389 and c_cont < best_c + 0.02:
           h = interval_to_h(raw_best, K, n)
           h = ensure_h(h)
           c = c5_eval(h, 2.0/n)
           # maybe coordinate polish quick? Here not; run can call coordinate_polish on returned h if improves.
           if c < best_c:
              best_h=h; best_c=c
    return best_h,best_c
```

`optimize_interval_K` code:
```
def optimize_interval_K(K, rng, phase_deadline):
    global _interval_state
    state = IntervalState(K, 600)
    _interval_state = state
    raw_best=None; c_best=1e100
    raws=[np.zeros(2*K+1)]
    for _ in range(2): raws.append(rng.normal(0.0,1.0,2*K+1))
    # also start from alternating? raw all zero uniform.
    for raw0 in raws:
       if time.time() > phase_deadline - 10: break
       raw = raw0.copy()
       # quick eval
       c = state.c_true(raw)
       if c < c_best: c_best=c; raw_best=raw.copy()
       try:
          res = minimize(_interval_obj, raw.copy(), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter': 120, 'xatol':1e-6, 'fatol':1e-8})
          if res.x is not None:
             c = float(res.fun) if np.isfinite(res.fun) else state.c_true(res.x)
             if c < c_best:
                c_best=c; raw_best=res.x.copy()
       except Exception:
          pass
    return raw_best, c_best
```

`interval_to_h`:
```
def interval_to_h(raw,K,n):
    s,e = interval_params_to_bounds(raw,K)
    dx=2.0/n
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*dx
    h=np.zeros(n)
    for j in range(K):
       h[(x >= s[j]) & (x < e[j])] = 1.0
    N=n//2
    diff = float(N - h.sum())
    if abs(diff) > 1e-9:
       if diff > 0:
          idxs = np.flatnonzero(h == 0)
       else:
          idxs = np.flatnonzero(h == 1)
       if idxs.size > 0:
          endpoints = np.concatenate([np.array(s), np.array(e)])
          dist = np.min(np.abs(x[idxs, None] - endpoints[None, :]), axis=1)
          order = idxs[np.argsort(dist)][:int(round(abs(diff)))]
          if diff > 0: h[order]=1.0
          else: h[order]=0.0
    # if still off due to rounding, project? avoid full projection; adjust a few
    diff2 = N - h.sum()
    if abs(diff2) > 1e-9:
       # use project_to_sum as fallback
       h = project_to_sum(h, float(N))
    return h
```
`np.diff` float maybe 0.999999; int(round(abs(diff))). If diff not integer due to float, okay.
If diff larger than idxs size, use all. Then fallback project.

```
def softmax_z(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=float)
    if z.size == 0: return np.zeros(0)
    a = z - float(np.max(z))
    e = np.exp(a)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0: return np.full(z.shape, 1.0/z.size)
    return e / s
```

`interval_params_to_bounds`:
```
def interval_params_to_bounds(raw, K):
    a = softmax_z(raw[:K])
    g = softmax_z(raw[K:])
    s = np.empty(K); e = np.empty(K)
    pos = float(g[0])
    for j in range(K):
        s[j] = pos
        ee = pos + float(a[j])
        e[j] = ee
        pos = ee + float(g[j+1])
    return s, e
```

`IntervalState`:
```
class IntervalState:
    def __init__(self,K,n_t):
       self.K=K; self.n_t=n_t
       self.t = np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_t,endpoint=True)
       self.two = 2.0 - self.t
    def c_true(self, raw):
       K=self.K; s,e = interval_params_to_bounds(raw,K)
       t=self.t; two=self.two
       P=np.zeros(self.n_t); R=np.zeros(self.n_t); Q=np.zeros(self.n_t)
       for j in range(K):
          sj=float(s[j]); ej=float(e[j])
          P += np.maximum(0.0, np.minimum(ej, two) - sj)
       for l in range(K):
          sl=float(s[l]); el=float(e[l])
          low = np.maximum(sl - t, 0.0)
          up = np.minimum(el - t, two)
          R += np.maximum(0.0, up - low)
       for j in range(K):
          sj=float(s[j]); ej=float(e[j])
          for l in range(K):
             sl=float(s[l]); el=float(e[l])
             low = np.maximum(sj, sl + t)
             up = np.minimum(ej, el + t)
             up = np.minimum(up, two)
             Q += np.maximum(0.0, up - low)
       vals = np.maximum(P - Q, R - Q)
       vals = np.maximum(vals, 0.0)
       return float(np.max(vals))
    def objective(self, raw):
       return self.c_true(raw)
```
